Appendix A
Maximal Symmetric Group of
\operatorname{MultiHead}

This section aims to provide a complete answer to the question of when two sets of parameters of a multi-head attention mechanism will define the same function. The answer to this question will serve as the core of the design principle of NFNs for Transformers. We recall the formulation of
\operatorname{Head}
and
\operatorname{MultiHead}
attention maps in Section
A.1
. Then, the maximal symmetric group of
\operatorname{Head}
is derived in Section
A.2
.

A.1
Multi-head Attention

Let
D
be a positive integer. Recall the notion of the parameterized map
\operatorname{Head}
as follows: For a positive integer
L
and
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Head}\left(X;W^{(Q)},W^{(K)},W^{(V)}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{softmax}\left(\frac{\left(XW^{(Q)}\right)\cdot\left(XW^{(K)}\right)^{\top}}{\sqrt{D_{k}}}\right)\cdot\left(XW^{(V)}\right),

where
W^{(Q)},W^{(K)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}
and
W^{(V)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{v}}
. By definition, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Head}\left(\cdot;W^{(Q)},W^{(K)},W^{(V)}\right)~\colon~\bigsqcup_{l>0}\mathbb{R}^{l\times D}\rightarrow\bigsqcup_{l>0}\mathbb{R}^{l\times D_{v}},

and for all
l>0
, the image of
\mathbb{R}^{l\times D}
is contained in
\mathbb{R}^{l\times D_{v}}
. By combining
\left(W^{(Q)}\right)\cdot\left(W^{(K)}\right)^{\top}/\sqrt{D_{k}}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we can rewrite the map
\operatorname{Head}
as follows

\displaystyle\operatorname{Head}\left(X;W^{(Q)},W^{(K)},W^{(V)}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{softmax}\left(X\cdot\frac{\left(W^{(Q)}\right)\cdot\left(W^{(K)}\right)^{\top}}{\sqrt{D_{k}}}\cdot X^{\top}\right)\cdot\left(XW^{(V)}\right),

By this observation, we define a class of parameterized maps as follows: For
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle f(X;A)\coloneqq\operatorname{softmax}\left(XAX^{\top}\right)\cdot X,

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
. Similarly, for the
\operatorname{MultiHead}
map, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{MultiHead}

\displaystyle\left(X;W^{(O)},\left\{W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\bigoplus\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{Head}\left(X;W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)}\right)\right)W^{(O)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{Head}\left(X;W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)}\right)W^{(O,i)}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{softmax}\left(\frac{\left(XW^{(Q,i)}\right)\cdot\left(XW^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}}{\sqrt{D_{k}}}\right)\cdot X\cdot\left(W^{(V,i)}\cdot W^{(O,i)}\right),

where
h
is a positive integer,
W^{(O)}=\left(W^{(O:1)},\ldots,W^{(O:h)}\right)
with each
W^{(O:i)}\in\mathbb{R}^{D_{v}\times D}
. Considering
W^{(V,i)}\cdot W^{(O,i)}
as a matrix
B_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we define a new class of parameterized maps as follows: For
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle F\left(X;\left\{A_{i},B_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{h}f(X;A_{i})\cdot B_{i},

where
h
is a positive integer and
A_{i},B_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
. Note that,

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}\left(\left(W^{(Q)}\right)\cdot\left(W^{(K)}\right)^{\top}/\sqrt{D_{k}}\right)\leqslant\min(D,D_{k})\leqslant D

\displaystyle\operatorname{rank}\left(W^{(V,i)}\cdot W^{(O,i)}\right)\leqslant\min(D,D_{v})\leqslant D

So, in general, the new class of
F
maps contains the class of
\operatorname{MultiHead}
maps. Note that,
F
simply is a weighted summation of some
f
maps and is linear with respect to
\{B_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
.

Remark 7
.

We can see, in
f
, the matrix
A
plays the role as a core matrix that defines
f
. Similarly, in
F
, each
A_{i}
defines each
f_{i}
, and
B_{i}
is the weight of each component contributes to
F
.

A.2
Maximal symmetric group of multi-head attention

In this section, we present a theoretical result that shows the following: Roughly speaking, in the multi-head scenario, each individual head plays its own unique role. The main results are Theorems
A.1
(which corresponds to Theorem
3.1
in the main text) on the unique role of each head and Theorem
A.7
(which corresponds to Theorem
3.2
in the main text) on the maximal symmetric group of multi-head attention.

Theorem A.1
.

Let
D
be a positive integer. Assume that for a positive integer
h
, matrices
A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
and
B_{1},B_{2},\ldots,B_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle F\left(X;\left\{A_{i},B_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)=\sum_{i=1}^{h}f(X;A_{i})\cdot B_{i}=0

(19)

for all positive integers
L
and
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
. Then, if
A_{1},A_{2},\ldots,A_{h}
are pairwise distinct, then

\displaystyle B_{1}=\ldots=B_{h}=0.

Proof.

From Equation (
19
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{softmax}\left(XA_{i}X^{\top}\right)\cdot X\cdot B_{i}=0

(20)

Consider
L>1
. We write
X=(x,y,y,\ldots,y)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
where
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})
and
y=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{D})
in
\mathbb{R}^{1\times D}
(So in
X
,
y
appears
L-1
times). We will consider the entry in the first row and first column of both sides of Equation (
20
). Let
b_{1},b_{2},\ldots,b_{h}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times 1}
be the first column of matrices
B_{1},B_{2},\ldots,B_{h}
. From Equation (
20
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(\dfrac{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot x+\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot y\right)\cdot b_{i}=0

(21)

By substituting
x=y=(1,0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
to Equation (
21
), we have the sum of all first entries of
b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}
is equal to
0
. Similarly, for every
j=1,\ldots,D
, the sum of
j^{\text{th}}
entries of
b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}
is equal to
0
. It shows that

\displaystyle b_{1}+b_{2}+\ldots+b_{h}=0.

(22)

From Equation (
21
), we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(\dfrac{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot x+\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot y\right)\cdot b_{i}

(23)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(\dfrac{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot x+\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot(y-x)\right)\cdot b_{i}

(24)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}\left(x+\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot(y-x)\right)\cdot b_{i}

(25)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{h}x\cdot b_{i}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot(y-x)\cdot b_{i}

(26)

\displaystyle=x\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}b_{i}\right)+(y-x)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot b_{i}\right)

(27)

\displaystyle=(y-x)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}}+Le^{xA_{i}y^{\top}}}\cdot b_{i}\right)

(28)

\displaystyle=(y-x)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{L}{e^{xA_{i}x^{\top}-xA_{i}y^{\top}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)

(29)

\displaystyle=(y-x)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{L}{e^{xA_{i}(x-y)^{\top}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)

(30)

By let
z=x-y
, from the above equations, we have

\displaystyle z\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{L}{e^{xA_{i}z^{\top}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0,

(31)

or

\displaystyle z\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{xA_{i}z^{\top}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0,

(32)

for all
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})
and
z=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{D})
in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
. Now, each
xA_{i}z^{\top}
can be viewed as a polynomial in
2D
indeterminates
x_{1},\ldots,x_{D}
and
z_{1},\ldots,z_{D}
as follows

\displaystyle xA_{i}z^{\top}=\sum_{p=1}^{D}\sum_{q=1}^{D}(A_{i})_{p,q}\cdot x_{p}z_{q}.

(33)

Since
A_{1},\ldots,A_{h}
are pairwise distinct, so
xA_{1}z^{\top},\ldots,xA_{h}z^{\top}
are pairwise distinct polynomials. By Lemma
A.3
, there exists
u\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and a non-empty open set
V\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, such that for all
z\in V
, we have
uA_{1}z^{\top},\ldots,uA_{h}z^{\top}
are pairwise distinct real numbers. Obviously
0\notin V
. Now fix an
v\in V
and let
z=t\cdot v
for
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Denote
uA_{i}v^{\top}=s_{i}
, from Equation (
33
), we have

\displaystyle t\cdot v\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{i}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0

(34)

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, or

\displaystyle v\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{i}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0

(35)

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
. By continuity, this still holds for
t=0
. So we have

\displaystyle v\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{i}}+L}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0

(36)

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
. Now, consider the set

\displaystyle S\coloneqq\left\{\left(\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{1}}+L},\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{2}}+L},\ldots,\dfrac{1}{e^{t\cdot s_{h}}+L}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{h}~\colon~t\in\mathbb{R}\right\}.

(37)

From Equation (
36
), we have the linear span of
S
, i.e.
\operatorname{span}(S)
, satisfies that: For all
s=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{h})

\in\operatorname{span}(S)
, we have

\displaystyle v\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}s_{i}\cdot b_{i}\right)=0.

(38)

In other words,
v
and
\sum_{i=1}^{h}s_{i}\cdot b_{i}
are orthogonal to each other as two vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
. Since
s_{1},\ldots,s_{h}\in\mathbb{R}
are pairwise distinct, by Lemma
A.4
, there exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}\in\mathbb{R}
such that their
h
corresponding vectors in
S
form a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{h}
. This implies
\operatorname{span}(S)=\mathbb{R}^{h}
. We have the set

\displaystyle\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{h}s_{i}\cdot b_{i}~\colon~s=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{h})\in\mathbb{R}^{h}\right\}

(39)

\displaystyle=\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{h}s_{i}\cdot b_{i}~\colon~s=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{h})\in\operatorname{span}(S)\right\}.

(40)

By the previous observation, it implies that
v
is orthogonal to every vectors in
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)
. In other words, the orthogonal complement of
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)
, which is denoted by
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)^{\perp}
, contains
v
. This holds for every vectors
v
in the non-empty open set
V
, so

\displaystyle V\subset\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)^{\perp}.

(41)

Since
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)^{\perp}
is a linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{D}
that contains a non-empty open set of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so by Lemma
A.2
, we have
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)^{\perp}=\mathbb{R}^{D}
. This implies
\operatorname{span}\left(\{b_{1},\ldots,b_{h}\}\right)=0
, which means
b_{1}=\ldots=b_{h}=0
. So the first column of
B_{1},\ldots,B_{h}
are all equal to
0
, and a similar proof is applied for every other columns of
B_{1},\ldots,B_{h}
. So
B_{1}=\ldots=B_{h}=0
.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
D
be a positive integer, and
V
is a non-empty open set of
\mathbb{R}^{D}
with the usual topology. If
U
is a linear subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{D}
that contains
V
, then
U=\mathbb{R}^{D}
.

Proof.

Since
V
is non-empty, let
x\in V
. Since
V
is open, there exists
r>0
such that the closed ball

\displaystyle\bar{B}_{r}(x)=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{D}~\colon~\|x-y\|\leqslant r\}\subset V.

(42)

Then for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
that
y\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|x-\left(x+r\cdot\dfrac{y}{\|y\|}\right)\right\|=r,

(43)

which means

\displaystyle x+r\cdot\dfrac{y}{\|y\|}\in\bar{B}_{r}(x)\subset V\subset U.

(44)

Since
x
is also in
V\subset U
, and
U
is a linear subspace, then
y\in U
. So, for
y\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
that
y\neq 0
, we have
y\in U
, and clearly
0\in U
, so
U=\mathbb{R}^{D}
.
∎

Remark 8
.

Lemma
A.2
still holds if we replace
\mathbb{R}^{D}
by a normed vector space.

Lemma A.3
.

Let
n,h
be two positive integers, and
f_{1},\ldots,f_{h}\in\mathbb{R}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}]
be
h
pairwise distinct real polynomials in
n
variables. Then there exists a non-empty open subset
U
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
f_{1}(x),\ldots,f_{h}(x)
are pairwise distinct for all
x\in U
.

Proof.

Consider the polynomial

\displaystyle f=\prod_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant h}(f_{i}-f_{j})\in\mathbb{R}[x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}].

(45)

Since
f_{1},\ldots,f_{h}
are pairwise distinct, then
f_{i}-f_{j}
is non-zero for all
1\leqslant i<j\leqslant h
. It is well-known that the real polynomial ring is an integral domain, so
f
is non-zero. There exists
a\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
f(a)\neq 0
. Since
\mathbb{R}
is Hausdorff, we can choose a open set
V
of
\mathbb{R}
that contains
a
and does not contain
0
. Let
U=f^{-1}(V)
, and since
f
is continuous,
U
is open. We have
f(x)\neq 0
for all
x\in U
, which means
f_{1}(x),\ldots,f_{n}(x)
are pairwise distinct for all
x\in U

∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
h
be a positive integer, and
h
distinct real numbers
s_{1},\ldots,s_{h}
. Then there exists
h
real numbers
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
and a positive integer
L
such that the matrix

\displaystyle A=(a_{ij})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant h,1\leqslant j\leqslant h}\in\mathbb{R}^{h\times h},~~\text{ where }a_{ij}=\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}+L},

(46)

is full rank. In other words, there exists
h
real numbers
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
and a positive integer
L
such that
h
vectors

\displaystyle\left(\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{1}}+L},\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{2}}+L},\ldots,\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{h}}+L}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{h}

(47)

for
i=1,\ldots,h
, form a basis of
\mathbb{R}^{h}
.

Proof.

We first have an observations. Let
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
be fixed and
x
be a variable. Consider the matrix

\displaystyle A(x)=(a_{ij})_{1\leqslant i\leqslant h,1\leqslant j\leqslant h},~~\text{ where }a_{ij}=\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}+x}

(48)

Then the determinant of
A(x)
, denoted by
\operatorname{det}(A(x))
can be viewed as a real rational function, i.e. a function that can be written as the ratio of two real polynomials. So, in the case that this rational function is zero,
\operatorname{det}(A(x))=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
, and in the case that this rational function is non-zero, there are a finite number of
x\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\operatorname{det}(A(x))=0
or
\operatorname{det}(A(x))
is not defined. In other words,
A(x)
is not full rank for all
x
, or for only a finite number of
x
. So, in Lemma
A.4
, if there exists
h
real numbers
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
and a real number
L
that makes
A
becomes full rank, then
L
can be made to be a positive integer.

Back to the problem, we will prove by mathematical induction. We will show that for every
h
, it is possible to choose
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
and
L
to make
\operatorname{det}(A)
becomes non-zero. For
h=1
, then the matrix
A
is full rank since its single entry is always positive for
t=L=1
. Assume that the result holds for a positive integer
h-1
, we will show it holds for
h
. For
j=1,\ldots,h
, let
B_{j}
is the
(h-1)\times(h-1)
matrix obtained by removing the first row and the
j^{\text{th}}
column of matrix
A
. By computing the determinant of
A
via the Laplace expansion along the first row, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{det}(A)=\sum_{j=1}^{h}(-1)^{1+j}\cdot a_{1j}\cdot\operatorname{det}(B_{j})=\sum_{j=1}^{h}(-1)^{1+j}\cdot\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+2}\cdot\operatorname{det}(B_{j})

(49)

Denote
c_{j}=(-1)^{1+j}\cdot\operatorname{det}(B_{j})
, and note that
c_{j}
depends on the choice of
t_{2},\ldots,t_{h}
. Without loss of generality, assume
s_{1}\neq 0
. Since
s_{2},\ldots,s_{h}
are
h-1
pairwise distinct real numbers, by the induction hypothesis, there exists
t_{2},\ldots,t_{h}
such that
c_{1}
is non-zero for at least one
L\in\mathbb{R}
. So with this choice of
t_{2},\ldots,t_{h}
, there exists
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle c_{1}\text{ is non-zero for all }L<\alpha.

(50)

Since
s_{1},\ldots,s_{h}
are pairwise distinct and
s_{1}\neq 0
, we can choose
t=t_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
such that:

1.

e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}>1-\alpha
; and,

2.

|e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}-e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}|>3
; for all
(i,j)\neq(1,1)
.

With this choice of
t_{1}
, let
\Delta=[-1-e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}},1-e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}]
. Then for
L\in\Delta

1.

We have
e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}+L\in[-1,1]
.

2.

For
(i,j)\neq(1,1)
, since
|e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}-e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}|>3
, then
e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}+L\notin[-1,1]
.

3.

We have
L\leqslant 1-e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}<1-(1-\alpha)=\alpha
.

We show that with this choice of
t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{h}
, there exists
L\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\operatorname{det}(A)
is non-zero. Assume the contrary that

\sum_{j=1}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+L}\cdot c_{j}=0

(51)

for all
L\in\mathbb{R}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}+L}\cdot c_{1}=\sum_{j=2}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+L}\cdot c_{j},

(52)

so

\displaystyle\left|\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}+L}\right|\cdot\left|c_{1}\right|=\left|\sum_{j=2}^{h}\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+L}\cdot c_{j}\right|\leqslant\sum_{j=2}^{h}\left|\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+L}\right|\cdot\left|c_{j}\right|

(53)

Considering
c_{1},\ldots,c_{h}
as functions in
L
, we have these functions are well-defined on the closed interval
\Delta
, since they are determinants of matrices where their entries are

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{i}\cdot s_{j}}+L}

(54)

for
1<i\leqslant h
and
1\leqslant j\leqslant h
, and these entries are defined on
\Delta
by the choice of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
. Moreover,
c_{1},\ldots,c_{h}
are continuous on
\Delta
. Since a continuous function on a compact set is bounded, so there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle|c_{1}|,\ldots,|c_{h}|<\delta_{1},~\text{ for all }L\in\Delta.

(55)

Moreover, since
L\in\Delta
implies
L<\alpha
, then
|c_{1}|>0
for all
L<\alpha
, which means there exists
\delta_{2}>0
such that

\displaystyle|c_{1}|>\delta_{2},~\text{ for all }L\in\Delta.

(56)

Similarly, for
1<j\leqslant h
,

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{j}}+L},

(57)

considered as functions in
L
, is well-defined and continuous on
\Delta
, so there exist
\delta_{3}>0
such that

\displaystyle\left|\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{2}}+L}\right|,\ldots,\left|\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{h}}+L}\right|<\delta_{3}~\text{ for all }L\in\Delta.

(58)

From Equation (
53
), since we have Equations (
55
), (
56
), (
58
), then for all
L\in\Delta\setminus\{-e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}\}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{2}\cdot\left|\dfrac{1}{e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}+L}\right|\leqslant(h-1)\cdot\delta_{1}\cdot\delta_{3}.

(59)

As
L\rightarrow-e^{t_{1}\cdot s_{1}}
, the LHS of Equation (
59
) goes to
\infty
, but the RHS is a constant, which is a contradiction. So with the choice of
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
, there exists
L\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\operatorname{det}(A)\neq 0
. The result holds for
h
. By mathematical induction, it holds for every positive integers
h
. The proof is done.
∎

Remark 9
.

If we fix a positive integer
L
, there might not exist
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
satisfy the condition. For instance, if
h\geqslant 4
, and
s_{1}+s_{2}=s_{3}+s_{4}=0
, then the matrix
A
is not full rank for all
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
.

We have two direct corollaries of Theorem
A.1
.

Corollary A.5
.

Let
D
be a positive integer. Assume that, for two positive integers
h,h^{\prime}
, collections
\{A_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
,
\{A^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}
,
\{B_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h}
,
\{B^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}
of matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle F\left(X;\left\{A_{i},B_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)=F\left(X;\left\{A^{\prime}_{i},B^{\prime}_{i}\right\}_{i=1}^{h^{\prime}}\right)

(60)

for all positive integers
L
and
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
. Then, for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i~:~A_{i}=A}B_{i}=\sum_{i~:~A^{\prime}_{i}=A}B^{\prime}_{i}.

(61)

Proof.

From Equation (
60
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{A\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}}f(XAX^{\top})\cdot X\cdot\left(\sum_{i~:~A_{i}=A}B_{i}-\sum_{i~:~A^{\prime}_{i}=A}B^{\prime}_{i}\right)=0,

(62)

then the result is directly from Corollary
A.5
.
∎

Corollary A.6
.

Let
D,D_{k},D_{v}
and
h,h^{\prime}
be positive integers. For
1\leqslant i\leqslant h
and
1\leqslant j\leqslant h^{\prime}
, let

\displaystyle\left(W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)},W^{(O,i)}\right)

(63)

\displaystyle\left(\overline{W}^{(Q,j)},\overline{W}^{(K,j)},\overline{W}^{(V,j)},\overline{W}^{(O,j)}\right)

(64)

be elements of
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{v}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D_{v}\times D}
. Assume that, the two corresponding MultiHead’s are identical, i.e.

\displaystyle\operatorname{MultiHead}\left(X;\left\{W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)},W^{(O,i)}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\operatorname{MultiHead}\left(X;\left\{\left(\overline{W}^{(Q,j)},\overline{W}^{(K,j)},\overline{W}^{(V,j)},\overline{W}^{(O,j)}\right)\right\}_{j=1}^{h^{\prime}}\right).

(65)

for all positive integers
L
and
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
. Then, for all
A\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i~:~W^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left(W^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}=A}W^{(V,i)}\cdot W^{(O,i)}=\sum_{j~:~\overline{W}^{(Q,j)}\cdot\left(\overline{W}^{(K,j)}\right)^{\top}=A}\overline{W}^{(V,j)}\cdot\overline{W}^{(O,j)}.

(66)

We characterize the symmetries of the weights of
\operatorname{MultiHead}
in the following theorem.

Theorem A.7
.

Let
h,D,D_{k},D_{v}
be positive integers.
Let
\left(W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)},W^{(O,i)}\right)
and
\left(\overline{W}^{(Q,i)},\overline{W}^{(K,i)},\overline{W}^{(V,i)},\overline{W}^{(O,i)}\right)
be arbitrary elements of
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{v}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D_{v}\times D}
with
i=1,\ldots,h
.
Assume that

(a)

\max(D_{k},D_{v})\leqslant D
,

(b)

the matrices
W^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left(W^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}
,
\overline{W}^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left(\overline{W}^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}
,
W^{(V,i)}\cdot W^{(O,i)}
, and
\overline{W}^{(V,i)}\cdot\overline{W}^{(O,i)}
are of full rank,

(c)

the matrices
W^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left(W^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}
with
i=1,\ldots,h
are pairwise distinct,

(d)

the matrices
\overline{W}^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left(\overline{W}^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}
with
i=1,\ldots,h
are pairwise distinct.

Then the following are equivalent:

1.

For every positive integer
L
and every
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
, we always have

\displaystyle\operatorname{MultiHead}\left(X;\left\{W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)},W^{(O,i)}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\operatorname{MultiHead}\left(X;\left\{\overline{W}^{(Q,i)},\overline{W}^{(K,i)},\overline{W}^{(V,i)},\overline{W}^{(O,i)}\right\}_{i=1}^{h}\right).

2.

There exist matrices
M^{(i)}\in\operatorname{GL}_{D_{k}}(\mathbb{R})
and
N^{(i)}\in\operatorname{GL}_{D_{v}}(\mathbb{R})
for each
i=1,\ldots,h
, as well as a permutation
\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{h}
, such that

\displaystyle\left(\overline{W}^{(Q,\tau(i))},\overline{W}^{(K,\tau(i))},\overline{W}^{(V,i)},\overline{W}^{(O,\tau(i))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\left(W^{(Q,i)}\cdot(M^{(i)})^{\top},W^{(K,i)}\cdot(M^{(i)})^{-1},W^{(V,i)}\cdot N^{(i)},(N^{(i)})^{-1}\cdot W^{(O,i)}\right).

Proof.

The implication
(2.)\Rightarrow(1.)
is clear.
Let us consider the implication
(1.)\Rightarrow(2.)
.
For each
s=1,\ldots,h
, we set

\displaystyle A^{(s)}

\displaystyle=W^{(Q,s)}\cdot\left(W^{(K,s)}\right)^{\top},

\displaystyle\overline{A}^{(s)}

\displaystyle=\overline{W}^{(Q,s)}\cdot\left(\overline{W}^{(K,s)}\right)^{\top},

\displaystyle B^{(s)}

\displaystyle=W^{(V,s)}\cdot W^{(O,s)},

\displaystyle\overline{B}^{(s)}

\displaystyle=\overline{W}^{(V,s)}\cdot\overline{W}^{(O,s)},

which are matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
.
By applying Corollary
A.6
for
A=A^{(s)}
, we see that

\displaystyle B^{(s)}=\sum_{j~:~\overline{A}^{(j)}=A^{(s)}}\overline{B}^{(j)}.

(67)

However, since the matrices
\overline{A}^{(j)}
with
j=1,\ldots,h
are pairwise distinct, there exist a unique index
j_{s}\in\{1,\ldots,h\}
such that
\overline{A}^{(j_{s})}=A^{(s)}
.
The correspondence
s\mapstochar\rightarrow j_{s}
is a permutation of
\{1,\ldots,h\}
.
Therefore, we can write
j_{s}=\tau(s)
for some
\tau\in\mathcal{S}_{h}
.
Hence,
\overline{A}^{(\tau(s))}=A^{(s)}
, and thus equation
67
becomes

\overline{B}^{(\tau(s))}=B^{(s)}.

The theorem is then followed by the rank factorization of the matrices
A^{(s)}
and
B^{(s)}

(
Piziak & Odell, 1999
)
.
∎

Appendix B
Matrix Group Preserved by
\operatorname{LayerNorm}

In our setting, layer normalization is a row-wise operator.
In particular, for a row vector
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
, the standard layer normalization of
x
, denoted by
\operatorname{LayerNorm}(x)
, is determined as

\displaystyle\operatorname{LayerNorm}(x)=\sqrt{D}\cdot\frac{x-\bar{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}}{||x-\bar{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}||_{2}},

(68)

where
\bar{x}=\frac{1}{D}(x_{1}+\ldots+x_{D})
is the mean of the coordinates of
x
and
\mathbf{1}_{D}
is a row vector in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
whose coordinates are all equal to
1
.
Geometrically, we can view
\operatorname{LayerNorm}
as the composition

\displaystyle\operatorname{LayerNorm}=\tau\circ\rho,

(69)

of two transformations
\tau
and
\rho
on
\mathbb{R}^{D}
defined belows.

•

The perpendicular projection map
\rho\colon\mathbb{R}^{D}\rightarrow\left<\mathbf{1}_{D}\right>^{\perp}
which maps
x\mapstochar\rightarrow x-\bar{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}
for each row vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
.
Here, the hyperplane
\left<\mathbf{1}_{D}\right>^{\perp}
is the orthogonal complement of
\left<\mathbf{1}_{D}\right>
in
\mathbb{R}^{D}
with respect to the standard dot product. It is noted that
\rho
is a linear map with the kernel
\operatorname{Ker}(\rho)=\left<\mathbf{1}_{D}\right>
, which is the one-dimensional vector subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{D}
generated by the row vector
\mathbf{1}_{D}
.

•

The scaling map
\tau\colon\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\{0\}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{D}
which maps
x\mapstochar\rightarrow\sqrt{D}\frac{x}{||x||_{2}}
.
The image of
\tau
is the sphere centered at the origin of radius
\sqrt{D}
.

It is noted that, the
\operatorname{LayerNorm}
operator is not defined on the whole
\mathbb{R}^{D}
.
Indeed, since
\tau
is not defined at the origin, it is necesarily that
\rho(x)\neq 0
.
This means that
x
should not lie in the kernel of
\rho
.
Therefore, the
\operatorname{LayerNorm}
operator actually defines a map from the set
\mathbb{R}^{D}\setminus\left<\mathbf{1}_{D}\right>
to
\mathbb{R}^{D}
.

Under the view of the
\operatorname{LayerNorm}
as a composition above, it is natural to ask which matrix (or linear map represented by this matrix) will commute with both the projection
\rho
and the scaling
\tau
. The following theorem gives a complete answer to this question.

Theorem B.1
.

Let
D
be a positive integer.
Let
\rho
and
\tau
be the projection and scaling map defined above.
The following are equivalent for an arbitrary matrix
M\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})
:

1.

M
commutes with
\rho
and
\tau
, i.e.
\rho(xM)=\rho(x)M
and
\tau(xM)=\tau(x)M
for all row vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
.

2.

M
is an orthogonal matrix such that its row sums and column sums are all equal.

Proof.

One can direct verify (2.) implies (1.). To show (1.) implies (2.), let
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{D})\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
and
M=(m_{ij})_{1\leqslant i,j\leqslant D}\in\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}
. From
\rho(xM)=\rho(x)M
, we have

\displaystyle xM-\overline{xM}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}=(x-\overline{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D})M,

(70)

which means

\displaystyle\overline{xM}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}=\overline{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}\cdot M.

(71)

So

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}\left(x_{i}\cdot\left(\sum_{j=1}^{D}m_{ij}\right)\right)\right)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}x_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}m_{i1},~\sum_{i=1}^{D}m_{i2},~\ldots,~\sum_{i=1}^{D}m_{iD}\right).

(72)

It implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{D}x_{i}\cdot\left(\sum_{j=1}^{D}m_{ij}\right)=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}x_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}m_{i1}\right)=\ldots=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}x_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{D}m_{iD}\right).

(73)

The above equation holds for all feasible
x
(as
\rho
and
\tau
are not defined on the whole
\mathbb{R}^{d}
). But since the set of feasible
x
is dense in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, by continuity, the equation holds for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. It implies row sums and column sums of
M
are all equal.

In addition, since
\tau(xM)=\tau(x)M
, we have
\|xM\|_{2}=\|x\|_{2}
. By the same argument, it holds for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Hence,
M
is orthogonal.
∎

Remark 10

(generalized doubly stochastic matrix)
.

A matrix whose row sums and column sums are all equal to one is called a
generalized doubly stochastic matrix
.

Smoktunowicz et al. (2019)
characterized all orthogonal generalized doubly stochastic matrices. In particular, let
\operatorname{O}(D)
is the set of
D\times D
orthogonal matrices, and
\mathcal{U}_{D}
is the subset of
\operatorname{O}(D)
consists of all orthogonal matrices with the first column is
(1/\sqrt{D})\cdot(\mathbf{1}_{D})^{\top}
, then every orthogonal generalized doubly stochastic matrix can be writen in the form

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}1&0^{\top}\\
0&X\end{pmatrix}

(74)

for some
(D-1)\times(D-1)
orthogonal matrix
X
.

There are invertible matrices that do not permute
\phi
and
\tau
, but still have nice behavior with
\operatorname{LayerNorm}
, as we see in the following theorem.

Theorem B.2
.

Let
D
be a positive integer.
Then for arbitrary permutation matrix
P\in\mathcal{P}_{D}
and real number
\lambda\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{LayerNorm}(\lambda xP)=\operatorname{sign}(\lambda)\operatorname{LayerNorm}(x)P,

(75)

for every row vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{D}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\operatorname{LayerNorm}(\lambda xP)

\displaystyle=\tau\circ\rho(\lambda xP)

(76)

\displaystyle=\tau(\lambda xP-\overline{\lambda xP}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D})

(77)

\displaystyle=\tau(\lambda xP-\lambda\overline{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D}\cdot P)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sign}(\lambda)\tau(x-\overline{x}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{D})P

(78)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{sign}(\lambda)\operatorname{LayerNorm}(x)P

(79)

We finish the proof.
∎

Appendix C
Weight Space and Group Action on Weight Space

In this section, we recall the weight space of a transformer block and the group action on it. Then, we prove Theorem
C.5
(which corresponds to Theorem
4.3
in the main text).

C.1
Weight space

Recall that a standard transformer block, which is denoted by
\operatorname{Attn}
, is defined as follows: for each
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attn}(X)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\left(\operatorname{ReLU}(\hat{X}\cdot W^{(A)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot b^{(A)})\cdot W^{(B)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot b^{(B)}\right),

(80)

where

\displaystyle\hat{X}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\left(\operatorname{MultiHead}(X;\{W^{(Q,i)},W^{(K,i)},W^{(V,i)},W^{(O,i)}\}_{i=1}^{h})\right).

(81)

with
\mathbf{1}_{L}=[1,\ldots,1]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times 1}
.

The weight space
\mathcal{U}
of the above transformer block is the vector space:

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}=\left(\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{k}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{v}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D_{v}\times D}\right)^{h}\times\big(\mathbb{R}^{D\times D_{A}}\times\mathbb{R}^{D_{A}\times D}\big)\times\big(\mathbb{R}^{1\times D_{A}}\times\mathbb{R}^{1\times D}\big).

(82)

An element
U\in\mathcal{U}
is of the form

\displaystyle U=\Bigl(\left([W]^{(Q,i)},[W]^{(K,i)},[W]^{(V,i)},[W]^{(O,i)}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,h},\left([W]^{(A)},[W]^{(B)}\right),\left([b]^{(A)},[b]^{(B)}\right)\Bigr).

(83)

To emphasize the weights of
\operatorname{Attn}
, we will write
\operatorname{Attn}(X;U)
instead of
\operatorname{Attn}(X)
.

C.2
Group action on weight space

Consider the weight space
\mathcal{U}
defined in Equation (
82
) and Equation (
83
), set

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}=\operatorname{S}_{h}\times\operatorname{GL}_{D_{k}}(\mathbb{R})^{h}\times\operatorname{GL}_{D_{v}}(\mathbb{R})^{h}\times\mathcal{P}_{D}\times\mathcal{P}_{D_{A}}.

(84)

Each element
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
has the form

\displaystyle g=\Bigl(\tau,(M_{i})_{i=1,\ldots,h},(N_{i})_{i=1,\ldots,h},P_{\pi_{O}},P_{\pi_{A}}\Bigr),

(85)

where

\tau,\pi_{O},\pi_{A}
are permutations and
M_{i},N_{i}
are invertible matrices of appropriate sizes.

Definition C.1

(Group action on
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
)
.

The action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
on
\mathcal{U}
is defined to be a map
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}\times\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}

which maps an element
(g,U)\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}\times\mathcal{U}
with
U
and
g
given in Equation (
83
) and Equation (
85
)
to the element

\displaystyle gU

\displaystyle=\biggl(\left([gW]^{(Q,i)},[gW]^{(K,i)},[gW]^{(V,i)},[gW]^{(O,i)}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,h},

(86)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left([gW]^{(A)},[gW]^{(B)}\right),\left([gb]^{(A)},[gb]^{(B)}\right)\biggr),

(87)

where

\displaystyle[gW]^{(Q,i)}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(Q,\tau(i))}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{\top},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(K,i)}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(K,\tau(i))}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(V,i)}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(V,\tau(i))}\cdot N^{(\tau(i))},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(O,i)}

\displaystyle=\left(N^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(O,\tau(i))}\cdot P_{\pi_{O}},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(A)}

\displaystyle=P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(A)}\cdot P_{\pi_{A}},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(B)}

\displaystyle=P_{\pi_{A}}^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(B)},

\displaystyle[gb]^{(A)}

\displaystyle=[b]^{(A)}\cdot P_{\pi_{A}},

\displaystyle[gb]^{(B)}

\displaystyle=[b]^{(B)}.

In addition to the above definition, we will also need the following ones for the construction of the equivariant and invariant maps later.

Definition C.2
.

With notations as above, for each
i=1,\ldots,h
, we denote:

\displaystyle[WW]^{(QK,i)}:=[W]^{(Q,i)}\cdot\left([W]^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top},\quad\text{and}\quad[WW]^{(VO,i)}:=[W]^{(V,i)}\cdot[W]^{(O,i)}.

(88)

Remark 11
.

The terms
[WW]^{(QK,i)}
and
[WW]^{(VO,i)}
are equivariant under the action of
g
, since

\displaystyle[gWgW]^{(QK,i)}

\displaystyle:=\left([gW]^{(Q,i)}\cdot[gW]^{(K,i)}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\left([W]^{(Q,{\tau(i)})}\cdot(M^{\tau(i)})^{\top}\right)\cdot\left([W]^{(K,{\tau(i)})}\cdot(M^{\tau(i)})^{-1}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(Q,{\tau(i)})}\cdot\left([W]^{(K,{\tau(i)})}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=[WW]^{(QK,{\tau(i)})},

and

\displaystyle[gWgW]^{(VO,i)}

\displaystyle:=[gW]^{(V,i)}\cdot[gW]^{(O,i)}

\displaystyle=\left([W]^{(V,{\tau(i)})}\cdot M^{(\tau(i))}\right)\cdot\left((M^{(\tau(i))})^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(O,{\tau(i)})}\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}\right)

\displaystyle=[W]^{(V,{\tau(i)})}\cdot[W]^{(O,{\tau(i)})}\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}

\displaystyle=[WW]^{(VO,{\tau(i)})}\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}.

Therefore, we can intuitively say that these terms are compatible under the action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

Proposition C.3
.

With notations as above, we have

\displaystyle[gWgW]^{(QK,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=[WW]^{(QK,{\tau(i)})}_{j,k},

\displaystyle[gWgW]^{(VO,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=[WW]^{(VO,{\tau(i)})}_{j,\pi_{O}(k)},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(Q,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\left[[W]^{(Q,\tau(i))}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{\top}\right]_{j,k},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(K,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\left[[W]^{(K,\tau(i))}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{-1}\right]_{j,k},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(V,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\left[[W]^{(V,\tau(i))}\cdot N^{(\tau(i))}\right]_{j,k},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(O,i)}

\displaystyle=\left[\left(N^{(\tau(i))}\right)^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(O,\tau(i))}\right]_{j,\pi_{O}(k)},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(A)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(A)}_{\pi_{O}(j),\pi_{A}(k)},

\displaystyle[gW]^{(B)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(B)}_{\pi_{A}(j),k},

\displaystyle[gb]^{(A)}_{k}

\displaystyle=[b]^{(A)}_{\pi_{A}(k)},

\displaystyle[gb]^{(B)}_{k}

\displaystyle=[b]^{(B)}_{k}.

for all appropriate indices
j
and
k
.

Proof.

This proposition follows immediately from the definition of the group action on
\mathcal{U}
.
∎

Lemma C.4
.

Let
n
be a positive integer.
The following are equivalent for a matrix
M\in\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{R})
:

1.

\operatorname{ReLU}(x\cdot M)=\operatorname{ReLU}(x)\cdot M
for all row vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

2.

M
is a monomial matrix whose nonzero entries are positive numbers.

Proof.

See
(
Godfrey et al., 2022
)
.
∎

Theorem C.5

(Invariance of
\operatorname{Attn}
under the action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
)
.

With notations as above, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attn}(X;gU)=\operatorname{Attn}(X;U)

(89)

for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
,
g\in\mathcal{G}
, and
X\in\mathbb{R}^{L\times D}
.

Proof.

According to Equation (
80
) and Equation (
81
), we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Attn}(X;gU)

(90)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\big(\operatorname{ReLU}(\hat{X}\cdot[gW]^{(A)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot[gb]^{(A)})\cdot[gW]^{(B)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot[gb]^{(B)}\big)

(91)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\big(\operatorname{ReLU}(\hat{X}\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(A)}P_{\pi_{A}}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot[b]^{(A)}P_{\pi_{A}})\cdot P_{\pi_{A}}^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(B)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}\cdot[b]^{(B)}\big)

(92)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\big(\operatorname{ReLU}(\hat{X}P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}[W]^{(A)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}[b]^{(A)})P_{\pi_{A}}P_{\pi_{A}}^{-1}[W]^{(B)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}[b]^{(B)}\big)

(93)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\big(\operatorname{ReLU}(\hat{X}P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}[W]^{(A)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}[b]^{(A)})[W]^{(B)}+\mathbf{1}_{L}[b]^{(B)}\big).

(94)

In the above equalities, Equation (
92
) following from the definition of the group action of
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
on
\mathcal{U}
, while Equation (
93
) follows from Lemma
C.4
.
In addition, we proceed the term
\hat{X}P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}
inside Equation (
94
) as:

\displaystyle\hat{X}P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}

(95)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\big(\operatorname{MultiHead}(X;\{[gW]^{(Q,i)},[gW]^{(K,i)},[gW]^{(V,i)},[gW]^{(O,i)}\}_{i=1}^{h})\big)\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}

(96)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{Head}(X;\{[gW]^{(Q,i)},[gW]^{(K,i)},[gW]^{(V,i)}\}_{i=1}^{h})[gW]^{(O,i)}\right)\cdot P_{\pi_{O}}^{-1}

(97)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{Head}\left(X;\{[W]^{(Q,\tau(i))},[W]^{(K,\tau(i))},[W]^{(V,\tau(i))}\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)[W]^{(O,\tau(i))}\right)

(98)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LayerNorm}\left(\sum\limits_{i=1}^{h}\operatorname{Head}\left(X;\{[W]^{(Q,i)},[W]^{(K,i)},[W]^{(V,i)}\}_{i=1}^{h}\right)[W]^{(O,i)}\right)

(99)

\displaystyle=\hat{X}.

(100)

In the above equalities, we used the notations

\displaystyle\overline{[W]}^{(Q,\tau(i))}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(Q,\tau(i))}\cdot(M^{(\tau(i))})^{\top},

\displaystyle\overline{[W]}^{(K,\tau(i))}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(K,\tau(i))}\cdot(M^{(Q,\tau(i))})^{-1},

\displaystyle\overline{[W]}^{(V,\tau(i))}

\displaystyle=[W]^{(V,\tau(i))}\cdot M^{(V,\tau(i))},

\displaystyle\overline{[W]}^{(O,\tau(i))}

\displaystyle=(M^{(V,\tau(i))})^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(O,\tau(i))}.

In the above equalities,
Equation (
98
) follows from Theorem
A.1
and Corollary
B.2
.
In addition, Equation (
99
) obtained by permuting terms in the sum by
\tau
.
From Equation (
94
) and Equation (
100
), we see that

\operatorname{Attn}(X;gU)=\operatorname{Attn}(X;U),

for all
X,g
and
U
.
The theorem is then proved.
∎

Appendix D
An Equivariant Polynomial Layer

We now proceed to construct a
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
-equivariant polynomial layer, denoted as
E
. These layers serve as the fundamental building blocks for our Transformer-NFNs. Our method is based on parameter sharing mechanism.

D.1
A general form with unknown coefficients

We want to build a map
E\colon\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
such that
E
is
\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
-equivariant.
We select
E(U)
to be a linear combination of entries of the matrices
[W]^{(Q,s)}
,
[W]^{(K,s)}
,
[W]^{(V,s)}
,
[W]^{(O,s)}
,
[W]^{(A)}
,
[W]^{(B)}
,
[b]^{(A)}
,
[b]^{(B)}
as well as the matrices
[WW]^{(QK,s)}
and
[WW]^{(VO,s)}
, i.e

\displaystyle E(U)

\displaystyle=\biggl(\left([E(W)]^{(Q,i)},[E(W)]^{(K,i)},[E(W)]^{(V,i)},[E(W)]^{(O,i)}\right)_{i=1,\ldots,h},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left([E(W)]^{(A)},[E(W)]^{(B)}\right),\left([E(b)]^{(A)},[E(b)]^{(B)}\right)\biggr),

(101)

where

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(Q,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k},

(102)

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(K,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(K,i):j,k},

(103)

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(V,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(V,i):j,k},

(104)

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(O,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(O,i):j,k},

(105)

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(A)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(A):j,k},

(106)

\displaystyle[E(W)]^{(B)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(B):j,k},

(107)

\displaystyle[E(b)]^{(A)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\phi^{(A):k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(A):k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(A):k},

(108)

\displaystyle[E(b)]^{(B)}_{k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[WW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[W]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(K,s):p,q}[W]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(V,s):p,q}[W]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(O,s):p,q}[W]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(B):k},

(109)

and the constants
\Phi^{-}_{-}
s are parameters.

In the following subsections, we will determine the constraints on the coefficients
\Phi^{-}_{-}
such that
E(gU)=gE(U)
for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
.

D.2
Auxiliary lemmas

We will need the following auxiliary lemmas in order to determine the constraints of the coefficients of
E
later.

Lemma D.1
.

Assume that
E\colon\mathcal{U}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}
is a function defined as in Equation (
101
) for some coefficients
\Phi^{-}_{-}
.
If
E(U)=0
for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
, then all coefficients are equal to zero.

Proof.

We view the entries of
U
as indeterminates over
\mathbb{R}
.
Under this view, since
E(U)=0
for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
, we can view
E(U)
as a zero polynomial.
Moreover, the set including all entries of the matrices
[W]^{(Q,s)}
,
[W]^{(K,s)}
,
[W]^{(V,s)}
,
[W]^{(O,s)}
,
[W]^{(A)}
,
[W]^{(B)}
,
[b]^{(A)}
,
[b]^{(B)}
as well as the matrices
[WW]^{(QK,s)}
and
[WW]^{(VO,s)}
is a linear independent set over
\mathbb{R}
.
Therefore, the coefficients of
E
, which are
\Phi^{-}_{-}
, must be equal to zero.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let
h
and
D
be positive integers.
Let
f^{(1)}_{s},\,f^{(2)}_{s}\colon\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be
\mathbb{R}
-linear functions for each
s=1,\ldots,h
.
Assume that there exists a constant
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)+f^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)=\lambda,

(110)

for all
\left(M^{(1)},\ldots,M^{(h)}\right)\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})^{h}
.
Then

f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M\right)=f^{(2)}_{s}\left(M\right)=\lambda=0

for all
s=1,\ldots,h
and
M\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

Fix an arbitrary element
\left(M^{(1)},\ldots,M^{(h)}\right)
of
\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})^{h}
.
Then for arbitrary nonzero numbers
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{h}
, the tuple

\left(\lambda_{1}M^{(1)},\ldots,\lambda_{h}M^{(h)}\right)
is also an element of
\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})^{h}
.
Since
f^{(1)}_{s},f^{(2)}_{s}
are linear, it follows from Equation (
110
) that

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\lambda_{s}f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)+\frac{1}{\lambda_{s}}\cdot f^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)=\lambda.

(111)

Consider the function

\displaystyle P(t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}):=\left(\prod\limits_{i=1}^{h}t_{i}\right)\cdot\left(\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}t_{s}f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)+\frac{1}{t_{s}}\cdot f^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)-\lambda\right)

(112)

as a polynomial in the variables
t_{1},\ldots,t_{h}
with coefficients in
\mathbb{R}
.
Then, according to Equation (
111
), we have
P(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{h})=0
for every nonzero numbers
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{h}
.
This happens only when
P
is a zero polynomial.
Thus all coefficients of
P
must be equal to zero.
In particular, we have
\lambda=0
and
f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)=f^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)=0
for all
s=1,\ldots,h
and all
\left(M^{(1)},\ldots,M^{(h)}\right)\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})^{h}
.
The lemma is then proved.
∎

Corollary D.3
.

Let
h
and
D
be a positive integers.
Let
f^{(1)}_{s},\,f^{(2)}_{s},g^{(1)}_{s},\,g^{(2)}_{s}\colon\mathbb{R}^{D\times D}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be linear functions for each
s=1,\ldots,h
.
Assume that there are constants
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that:

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}f^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)+f^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)+\lambda_{1}=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}g^{(1)}_{s}\left(M^{(s)}\right)+g^{(2)}_{s}\left(\left(M^{(s)}\right)^{-1}\right)+\lambda_{2},

(113)

for all
\left(M^{(1)},\ldots,M^{(h)}\right)\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})^{h}
.
Then
f^{(1)}_{s}(M)=g^{(1)}_{s}(M)
and
f^{(2)}_{s}(M)=g^{(2)}_{s}(M)
and
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}
for all
s=1,\ldots,h
and
M\in\operatorname{GL}_{D}(\mathbb{R})
.

Proof.

Apply Lemma
D.2
with
f^{(1)}_{s}
is replace by
f^{(1)}_{s}-g^{(1)}_{s}
,
f^{(2)}_{s}
is replace by
f^{(2)}_{s}-g^{(2)}_{s}
, and
\lambda
is replaced by
\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}
.
∎

D.3
Finding the constraints for the unknown coefficients

In the following, we will find necessary and sufficient conditions for the coefficients
\Phi^{-}_{-}
such that
E(gU)=gE(U)
for all
U\in\mathcal{U}
and
g\in\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{U}}
.
We follow the parameter-sharing strategy.
In particular, we first determine the entries of
E(gU)
and
gE(U)
.
Then, we compare the corresponding entries to determine the exact constraints on the coefficients.

D.3.1
Computing
E(gU)

Following Equation (
86
), we have

\displaystyle E(gU)

\displaystyle=\left(([E(gW)]^{(Q,i)},[E(gW)]^{(K,i)},[E(gW)]^{(V,i)},[E(gW)]^{(O,i)})_{i=1,\ldots,h},\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.([E(gW)]^{(A)},[E(gW)]^{(B)}),([E(gb)]^{(A)},[E(gb)]^{(B)})\right),

(114)

where

\displaystyle[E(gW)]^{(Q,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[gW]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[gW]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[gW]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[gW]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[gb]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[gb]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k},

(115)

\displaystyle[E(gW)]^{(K,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[gW]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[gW]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[gW]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[gW]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[gb]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(K,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[gb]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(K,i):j,k},

(116)

\displaystyle[E(gW)]^{(V,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(VO,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{(VO,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(Q,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(Q,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(K,s):p,q}[gW]^{(K,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(V,s):p,q}[gW]^{{(V,s)}}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(O,s):p,q}[gW]^{(O,s)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(A):p,q}[gW]^{(A)}_{p,q}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(B):p,q}[gW]^{(B)}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(A):q}[gb]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(V,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[gb]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(V,i):j,k},

(117)

\displaystyle[E(gW)]^{(O,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(O,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[gWgW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(QK,s):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,\tau(s))}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(Q,s):p,q}\left[[W]^{{(Q,\tau(s))}}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(s))}\right)^{\top}\right]_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{k}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(K,s):p,q}\left[[W]^{(K,\tau(s))}\cdot\left(M^{(\tau(s))}\right)^{-1}\right]_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{v}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(V,s):p,q}\left[[W]^{{(V,\tau(s))}}\cdot N^{(\tau(s))}\right]_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{v}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(O,s):p,q}\left[\left(N^{(\tau(s))}\right)^{-1}\cdot[W]^{(O,\tau(s))}\right]_{p,\pi_{O}(q)}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(A):p,q}[W]^{(A)}_{\pi_{O}(p),\pi_{A}(q)}+\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D_{A}}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(B):p,q}[W]^{(B)}_{\pi_{A}(p),q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(A):q}[b]^{(A)}_{\pi_{A}(q)}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(B):k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(B):k}.

(131)

Using the symmetries of the indices, we can rearrange the right hand sides of the above equations as:

\displaystyle[E(gW)]^{(Q,i)}_{j,k}

\displaystyle=\sum\limits_{s=1}^{h}\sum\limits_{p=1}^{D}\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(QK,\tau^{-1}(s)):p,q}[WW]^{{(QK,s)}}_{p,q}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D_{A}}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(A):\pi_{A}^{-1}(q)}[b]^{(A)}_{q}+\sum\limits_{q=1}^{D}\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k}_{(B):q}[b]^{(B)}_{q}+\Phi^{(Q,i):j,k},
